Review Process 
22 
Q. 
Please explain the Panel’s process for reviewing 
23 
the Company’s programs and projects. 
24 


--- Page 18 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
17 
 
A. 
We reviewed and evaluated the information 
1 
provided by the Company in its filing and 
2 
analyzed information provided in response to IRs 
3 
in this proceeding.  For each category of 
4 
Electric T&D: System Expansion and New Business, 
5 
Risk Reduction and Reliability, Replacement, 
6 
Equipment Purchases, and Environmental, and for 
7 
Electric Production, we analyzed the capital 
8 
project or program for which the Company 
9 
forecasted expenditures during the three rate 
10 
years.  We participated in numerous technical 
11 
discussions to investigate and confirm the 
12 
status of major on-going and conceptual capital 
13 
projects and to assist our analysis of the 
14 
timing, reasonableness of cost, and the need for 
15 
the projects.  When reviewing each project, 
16 
alternative analysis and options, such as non-
17 
wires alternatives and other potential solutions 
18 
were requested of the Company by the Panel and 
19 
included as part of our overall analysis.  
20 
Similarly, we reviewed programs where the 
21 
Company’s EIOP proposes an increase in funding.  
22 
This analysis and evaluation resulted in the 
23 
adjustments and recommendations discussed in our 
24

Case 25-E-0072

testimony. 
1 
Q. 
Given the Company’s proposed budgets for the 
2 
System Expansion and New Business category 
3 
included significant increases over prior years, 
4 
please explain the Panel’s approach to reviewing 
5 
the large substation projects identified in this 
6 
category and associated budgets? 
7 
A. 
In recent years the Company has experienced 
8 
significant load growth across its service 
9 
territory and specifically in the 
10 
Brooklyn/Queens load area where several new 
11 
substation projects have been proposed as well 
12 
as several substation projects that have already 
13 
been before the Commission in other proceedings.  
14 
For example, four of the eight major substation 
15 
projects identified in this case have already 
16 
been decided upon by the Commission in Cases 20-
17 
E-0197 and 22-E-0064 and total approximately 
18 
$1.03 billion of the approximate $2.01 billion 
19 
requested during the rate year for those eight 
20 
substation projects.  The remaining four 
21 
substation projects are new to this rate case 
22 
and total approximately $765 million in the rate 
23 
years.  The Company has identified each projects 
24 


--- Page 20 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
19 
 
need based on existing and projected load 
1 
forecasting that includes the business load and 
2 
electrification of new buildings and vehicles in 
3 
its service territory and Staff has reviewed the 
4 
timing, reasonableness and cost of those similar 
5 
to the other projects and programs proposed by 
6 
the Company.    
7

feeders.  With an increase in wire and equipment 
18

8

9

10 
on all primary distribution feeders.  Adequate 
11

the Company's primary and secondary distribution

14 
service.  Con Edison specifications require that 
15 
distribution feeders must operate within their

17 
continuous and contingency/ emergency operation.  
18 
Relief projects include replacing the overloaded 
19 
primary cable sections with a new primary cable 
20 
of higher rating, installing additional primary 
21 
cable sections in order to decrease the load on 
22 
the sections to a level where it is no longer 
23 
overloaded.

Q. 
Please describe the Company’s Network 
6 
Transformer Relief program. 
7 
A. 
The Network Transformer Relief program funds the 
8 
installation costs of providing relief to 
9 
network transformers whose projected load and 
10 
operating temperatures exceed their rating, per 
11 
Con Edison’s engineering specification EO-2002.  
12 
The relief work under this program consists of 
13

14 
transformer of a slightly higher rating, 
15 
replacing an existing transformer with a larger 
16 
transformer that has a significantly higher 
17 
rating, establishing a new vault and installing 
18 
a new transformer to reduce the loading on 
19 
nearby transformers, and mains to relieve 
20 
transformer overloads.  This program is an 
21 
annual program that has been included in prior 
22 
rate filings.

Q. 
Please describe the Company’s Circuit Switcher 
6 
Replacement Program. 
7 
A. 
This program will replace circuit switchers 
8 
installed in Con Edison Substations to improve 
9 
reliability.  The replacement is based on a 
10 
health ranking which is determined using 
11 
multiple factors including jumpers, service 
12 
type, station risk, SF6 emissions, O&M history, 
13 
model, age, and failure consequences.  According 
14 
to the Company, this program will upgrade or 
15 
replace three switches per year with a reliable 
16 
upgraded model.  As the program progresses, 
17 
other circuit switchers will be considered for 
18 
replacement based on performance, reliability, 
19 
and lack of replacement parts availability due 
20 
to obsolescence.  These are the switches that 
21 
have been identified as a priority. 
22

What funding did the Company request for this 
23

--- Page 103 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
102 
 
A. 
The Company requests capital investments of $2.8 
1 
million, $4.0 million and $4.12 million in RY1, 
2 
RY2, and RY3 respectively. 
3 
Q. 
What is the Company’s justification and need for 
4 
this program? 
5 
A. 
Circuit switchers function to provide electrical 
6 
isolation to substation equipment or 
7 
transmission lines during planned outages and/or 
8 
fault conditions.  If a circuit switcher does 
9 
not operate as intended, more equipment will 
10 
need to be isolated, than would otherwise be 
11 
required referred as overtripping.  Overtripping 
12 
can lead to contingencies that may cause 
13 
equipment overloads or require load shedding.  
14 
Replacing circuit switchers that are in poor 
15 
health will help maintain reliability and help 
16 
with climate change mitigation efforts.  Some 
17 
circuit switchers are targeted for replacement 
18 
because they are leaking SF6 gas, which is a 
19 
greenhouse gas (GHG). 
20 
Q. 
What funding does the Panel recommend this 
21 
program? 
22 
A. 
The Panel recommend $2.4 million for each of 
23 
RY1, RY2, and RY3.

Q. 
What is the Company’s justification and need for 
21

22 
A. 
Relay systems are critical in the protection of 
23 
electric transmission and distribution systems.  
24 


--- Page 108 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
107 
 
The reliable and timely operation of a relay 
1 
system electrically isolates a faulted piece of 
2 
equipment and protects the rest of the 
3 
transmission and distribution system from any 
4 
conditions that may cause further damage.  
5 
Legacy systems with known reliability issues on 
6 
the transmission system will continue to be 
7 
prioritized for replacement under this program.  
8 
A more standardized approach to upgrades, 
9 
however, and an expansion of the program to 
10 
include area station relays, (UFLS) Panel 
11 
upgrades, and TPL projects is critical.  The 
12 
standardized approach will help the Company 
13 
adapt to changing climate by improving 
14 
restoration times following extreme weather 
15 
events.  UFLS Panels are critical in gaining 
16 
faster recovery from decaying frequency to 
17 
maintain the balance between generation and load 
18 
followed by a major system disturbance.  
19 
Existing Clark relays associated with UFLS 
20 
design needs replacement due to their unreliable 
21 
operation, impacting our ability to shed the 
22 
load when needed.  Systemwide upgrade of UFLS 
23 
Panels is required to retire the early 
24 


--- Page 109 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
108 
 
generation of UFLS microprocessor relays.  
1 
Strengthening the components of UFLS Panel will 
2 
improve our ability to respond to system 
3 
imbalance and to prevent cascading events.  
4 
Modernizing relay systems by moving away from 
5 
legacy electromechanical and solid-state systems 
6 
towards microprocessor-based systems facilitates 
7 
real time data collection on health and the 
8 
ability to remotely recover from events.  
9 
Additionally, the newer microprocessors offer 
10 
increased intelligence and integrate advanced 
11 
technology to provide security against common 
12 
industry-wide issues leading to mis-operations. 
13

21

then the entire station could lose protection

--- Page 113 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
112 
 
for multiple elements simultaneously.  Con 
1

Q. 
What is the Company’s justification and need for 
3 
this program? 
4 
A. 
This program addresses critical infrastructure 
5 
that contain aging structural components such as 
6 
substation switchgear units, switchgear houses, 
7 
substation transformers, as well as new SCADA 
8 
capable flood and oil level sensors.  Structural 
9 
members of switchgear are aging, and these 
10 
factors have resulted in circuit breakers that 
11 
do not fit into their cubicles properly.  None 
12 
of the circuit breakers associated with these 
13 
switchgear units are currently manufactured, 
14 
requiring any repairs to be performed by a small 
15 
set of third-party specialist vendors, 
16 
introducing significant lead times and costly 
17 
repairs when the equipment is damaged.  Rather 
18 
than attempt to repair the structural problems, 
19 
this program funds the complete replacement of 
20 
the switchgear.  Upgrading the unit station 
21 
switchgear further increases system resiliency 
22 
by significantly improving Con Edison’s ability 
23 
to recover from outages more rapidly.  The 
24 


--- Page 117 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
116 
 
upgraded switches are self-contained, modular 
1

2 
individually, in less time than it takes to 
3 
replace legacy custom switchgear breakers.  
4 
Future replacements will also be faster and cost 
5 
less since they can be performed using Company 
6 
resources or by more broadly available contract 
7 
resources, rather than specialty vendors that are 
8 
in increasingly short supply.  All installations 
9

11 
“off-the-shelf” modular equipment that is more

readily available, minimizing the time needed to 
13 
acquire and install replacements when needed.  
14 
The upgrades will provide remote, secure access 
15 
to digital data to prioritize system restoration 
16 
while enhancing cybersecurity measures.  The 
17 
switchgear in scope of the Federal Emergency 
18 
Management Agency (FEMA) +5 guidelines will be

replaced in the unit substations on elevated

platforms that mitigate these flooding

vulnerabilities.  Installing the upgraded

3

4 
A.

5 
Exhibit___(EIOP-8) that this program will 
6

7 
found upon manhole leaks, follow-up work, or 
8 
structure inspections to prevent future 
9 
dielectric fluid leaks and increase feeder 
10

11 
in feeders being removed from service if the 
12

13 
the pressurization pumps.  If the operating 
14 
pressure cannot be maintained, failure of the 
15 
cable system can happen and require an extended 
16 
outage in order to complete repairs.  Even if 
17 
pressure can be maintained, a feeder with a 
18 
large leak may be forced out of service if order 
19 
repair the leak if the release of fluid cannot 
20 
be adequately controlled during the repair 
21 
process.

3

4 
A. 
In Exhibit___(EIOP-13), page 51, the Company 
5 
states Local Law 11, also known as the Façade 
6 
Inspection Safety Program (FISP), was enacted to 
7 
ensure the structural integrity and the safety 
8 
of building facades in the City of New York.  It 
9 
mandates that buildings over six stories undergo 
10 
façade inspection every five years and that 
11 
building owners perform any necessary repairs to 
12 
protect public safety.  The amendment to FISP 
13 
was implemented as part of Cycle 9, which began 
14 
in February 2020 through February 2025.  This 
15 
amendment introduced stricter requirements, 
16 
i.e., mandatory close-up inspections every 60 
17 
linear feet of building facades that face public 
18 
right-of-way, including sidewalks and easements.  
19 
These inspections often must be conducted from 
20 
scaffolding structures to physically examine the 
21 
potentially hazardous areas.  Additional changes 
22 
include mandatory cavity wall probes in specific 
23 
locations to assess the façade’s conditions and 
24 


--- Page 217 ---
Case 25-E-0072         
 
SEIOP 
 
 
216 
 
identify potential concealed defects.  These 
1 
changes aim to mitigate the risks of façade 
2 
debris falling and to ensure public safety.  Con 
3 
Edison, as property owner, must adapt these 
4 
revised inspection plans and budgets in 
5 
compliance with Local Law 11 requirements.

4

10

14 
for when new buildings are prohibited from 
15

16

17

19

set in rates.
